<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main-introduction}
Assume that \ref{a:W}, \ref{a:R} and
\textnormal{\ref{a:A1}--\ref{a:A5}} hold, and $\sigma(b) < q_X-\inf \Psi_X$.
Then:
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        {

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\item
$\sigma(b)>0$ and the function $W_V^{(-\sigma(b))}(\cdot, b)$
is strictly positive on $(b,\infty)$, and the measure
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax}

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:nu-introduction}
    \nu(dy)
    =
    \left(
        D^{(-\sigma(b))}(y)
        \mathbf{1}_{(b,0]}(y)
        +
        e^{-\Phi_X(q_X-\sigma(b))y}
        \mathbf{1}_{(0,\infty)}(y)
    \right)
    dy
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax}

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
is non-zero and has finite total mass.
\item
The function $W_V^{(-\sigma(b))}(\cdot,b)$ and the measure $\nu$ are
$\sigma(b)$-invariant for $Q$, in that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:right-left-invariance-introduction}
    Q_tW_V^{(-\sigma(b))}(\cdot,b)(x)
    =
    e^{-\sigma(b)t}W_V^{(-\sigma(b))}(x,b),
    \text{ and }
    \nu Q_t f = e^{-\sigma(b) t} \nu f,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
for every $x>b$ and bounded measurable function $f$.
\item
There exist functions $\psi_1,\psi_2$ (with the property that
$\lim_{x\to\infty} \psi_1(x) = \infty$) and constants
$C,\gamma >0$ such that, for all probability
measures $\mu$ on $(b,\infty)$ with $\mu \psi_1<\infty$ and $\mu\psi_2>0$,

    \label{eq:main-asymptotic-introduction}
\Big\|
\mathbb{P}^\dag_\mu\!\big( V_t \in \,\cdot \mathbin{\big|} t < \tau_\partial \big)
- \frac{\nu(\cdot)}{\nu(b,\infty)}
\Big\|_{\mathrm{TV}(\psi_1)}
\leq C e^{-\gamma t} \frac{\mu\psi_1}{\mu\psi_2},

<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{two-sided-exit}
For $q\geq 0$ and $b<0<a$,
\[
\mathbb{E}_x\!\left(
e^{-q\tau_a^+};\,
\tau_a^+ < \tau_b^-\wedge \zeta
\right)
=
\frac{W_V^{(q)}(x,b)}{W_V^{(q)}(a,b)}.
\]
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{exist_resolvent}
For \(q\geq0\), \(b<0<a\), and any non-negative measurable function
\(f\),
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\label{gen_resolvent}
\mathbb{E}_x\!\left[
\int_0^{\tau_a^+ \wedge \tau_b^-}
e^{-qt} f(V_t)\,dt
\right]
=
\int_{(b,a)} f(y)\, r_{(b,a)}^{(q)}(x,y)\, dy,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
and the $q$-resolvent density of $V$ killed outside the interval $[b,a]$ is
\[
r_{(b, a)}^{(q)}(x, y) =
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        {\begin{cases}} 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\dfrac{W_V^{(q)}(x, b)}{W_V^{(q)}(a, b)}
\,W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a-y)
- W_X^{(q+q_X)}(x-y),
& y \in (0,a], \\[1.2em]
\dfrac{W_V^{(q)}(x, b)}{W_V^{(q)}(a, b)}
\,W_V^{(q)}(a,y)
- W_V^{(q)}(x,y),
& y \in [b,0).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        {

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
\]

<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:limit_potential}
For \(q\geq 0\), the \(q\)-resolvent density of the process \(V\)
killed upon first passage below \(b<0\) exists and is given by
\[
r^{(q)}_b(x, y) =
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        {\begin{cases}} 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\displaystyle 
\frac{e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)y}}{D^{(q)}(b)}W_{V}^{(q)}(x,b) \,  - W^{(q+q_X)}_X(x-y), 
& y \in (0,\infty), \\[2ex]
\displaystyle 
\frac{D^{(q)}(y)}{D^{(q)}(b)} \, W_{V}^{(q)}(x,b) - W_{V}^{(q)}(x,y), 
& y \in (b,0],
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        {\end{cases}} 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
\]
where $D^{(q)} \colon (-\infty,0) \to \mathbb{R}$ is given by
\[
D^{(q)}(y) = \frac{q+q_X}{\Phi_X(q+q_X)}\,W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-y)
+\iint \Big( W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(-y)\, - W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(u-y)\Big)\,e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v} \; \tilde{\Pi}_X(du , dv).
\]
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
To begin with, we see from \cite[equation~(8.11)]{Kyprianou2014} that,
for $a,v\ge 0$ and for $q\in\mathbb{R}$ such that $q+q_X\ge 0$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
  \label{eq:exit-problem-monotonicity}
  \frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a-v)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  =
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[ e^{-(q+q_X)\tau_v^+} ; \tau_v^+ < \tau_{v-a}^-\bigr]
  \le
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[ e^{-(q+q_X)\tau_v^+} ; \tau_v^+ < \tau_{v-a'}^-\bigr],
  \qquad \text{for } a\le a',
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
and so, using \cite[equation~(3.15)]{Kyprianou2014},
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
  \label{eq:WX-limit}
  \frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a-v)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  \mathbin{\nearrow}
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[ e^{-(q+q_X)\tau_v^+} ; \tau_v^+<\infty \bigr]
  = e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v},
  \qquad a\to\infty.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
We will show that, for all $y\le 0$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
  \label{eq:WV-limit}
  \lim_{a\to\infty} \frac{W_V^{(q)}(a,y)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  = D^{(q)}(y) \in (0,\infty),
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
and from these two statements, the claim about resolvents follows by a simple calculation.

Starting with the left-hand side of \eqref{eq:WV-limit},
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{dini-type}
  \frac{W_V^{(q)}(a,y)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  &=
  \frac{q+q_X}{\Phi_X(q+q_X)} W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-y)\nonumber \\
  & \quad {}
  + \iint \biggl( W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-y) \biggr. \nonumber\\
  & \qquad\qquad\quad 
  \biggl. {} - e^{\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}\frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a-v)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(u-y) \biggr)
  e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v} \tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
By \eqref{eq:WX-limit}, the integrand is a decreasing function of $a$,
and thus by dominated convergence, the limit as $a\to\infty$ is given by
$D^{(q)}(y)<\infty$.
Moreover, $W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-y)-W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(u-y) > 0$ for $u \in (-y,0)$,
so $D^{(q)}(y)>0$. This establishes \eqref{eq:WV-limit}.

Now, considering the resolvent density $r^{(q)}_{(b,a)}$ when $y>0$, we have
\[
  r_{(b,a)}^{(q)}(x,y)
  = \frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a-y)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  \frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}{W_V^{(q)}(a,b)}
  W_V^{(q)}(x,b)
  - W_X^{(q+q_X)}(x-y).
\]
The considerations above show that, for fixed $y$, the first and second ratio
are both increasing in $a$ and converge to our putative expression for
$r^{(q)}_b(x,y)$. Applying monotone convergence to the integral
\eqref{gen_resolvent} restricted to $(0,\infty)$ completes the proof for $y>0$.

Take now $y\le 0$. In this case we have
\[
  r_{(b,a)}^{(q)}(x,y)
  = \frac{W_V^{(q)}(a,y)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}
  \frac{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a)}{W_V^{(q)}(a,b)}
  W_V^{(q)}(x,b)
  - W_V^{(q)}(x,y).
\]
Now, we see that for fixed $y$, the first ratio is decreasing in $a$ to $D^{(q)}(y)$,
and the second is increasing to $1/D^{(q)}(b)$.
Take any $a_0>0$. This function of $y$ is dominated (for any $x$ and uniformly in $a\ge a_0$) by
$g(y) \coloneqq \frac{W_V^{(q)}(a_0,y)}{W_X^{(q+q_X)}(a_0)} \frac{1}{D^{(q)}(b)} W_V^{(q)}(x,b) - W_V^{(q)}(x,y)$.
Since $W_V^{(q)}$ is bounded on compacts, $\int_b^0 g(y)\, dy < \infty$, and therefore we can apply 
dominated convergence in the integral \eqref{gen_resolvent} over the domain $[b,0]$.
This completes the proof for $y\le 0$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:weighted-to-unweighted}
  For any $q\ge 0$, $x \in \mathbb{R}$, and $\epsilon > 0$,
                                                                                        
   
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{A-express}
    0 \le A(q, \epsilon, x) \coloneqq \iint \Big(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\Big)e^{-\epsilon v}\,\tilde\Pi_X(du,dv)
    < \infty.  
   
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
  and is integrable in $(b, 0).$
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We know, since it appears in the expression for $D^{(q)}(x)$, \eqref{eq:WV-limit} implies that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
     \label{eq:Dq_finite}
  \iint \left(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\right)e^{-\Phi_X(q)v}\,\tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv)<\infty.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Define
\[
f_{q,\epsilon}(u, v) \coloneqq e^{-\epsilon v}\left(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\right)\ge0,\qquad u\le 0, v\geq 0.
\]
Fix \(M>0\) and split the integral into the regions \(\{v\le M\}\) and \(\{v>M\}\).
For \(0\le v\le M\), we have \(e^{-\Phi_X(q)v}\ge e^{-\Phi_X(q)M}>0\). Therefore,
\[
\iint_{v\le M} f_{q,\epsilon}(u, v)\,\tilde\Pi_X(du,dv)
\le e^{\Phi_X(q)M}\iint_{v\leq M}  \left(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\right)e^{-\Phi_X(q)v}\,\tilde\Pi_X(du,dv),
\]
which is finite by \eqref{eq:Dq_finite}.
Next, take \(v>M\).
Since \(W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\ge 0\) for \(u<0\), we have
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
  \iint_{v> M} f_{q,\epsilon}(u, v)\,\tilde\Pi_X(du,dv)
  &\le W_Y^{(q)}(-x) \iint_{v> M} e^{-\epsilon v}\,\tilde\Pi_X(du,dv) \\
  &= \frac{W_Y^{(q)}(-x)}{\epsilon} \int_{(-\infty,M]} (e^{\epsilon w} - e^{-\epsilon M}) \, \Pi_X(d w)
  < \infty,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
where we performed the change of variables $w = u - v$ and used Tonelli's theorem.
This completes the proof of finiteness. 

Next, we focus on integrability. Indeed, the second term is integrable over the compact interval $[b,0]$ because the scale function is continuous. We now deal with the first term. Note that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        {\begin{multline*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \iint_{v\leq M} \bigl(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-x)\bigr) e^{-\Phi_X(q)v}
  \Pi_X(du-v) dv 
  \\
  \le 
  \iint \bigl(W_Y^{(q)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q)}(u-y)\bigr) e^{-\Phi_X(q)v}
  \Pi_X(du-v) dv,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@multline*} 
        {\end{multline*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@multline*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
which appears in the expression of $D^{(q)}$ in \cref{lem:limit_potential}, and is thereby integrable since $q$-resolvent is finite.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:Dq_analytic}
Let
\[
\mathcal{D} := \{\, q \in \mathbb{C} : \operatorname{Re}(q+q_X) > \inf \Psi_X \,\}.
\]
Then for each fixed $x\le 0$, the map $q \mapsto D^{(q)}(x)$ has an analytic extension in~$\mathcal{D}$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Write $D^{(q)}(x)=D^{(q)}_1(x)+D^{(q)}_2(x)$ where
\[
D^{(q)}_1(x) := \frac{q+q_X}{\Phi_X(q+q_X)}\,W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-x)
\]
and
\[
D^{(q)}_2(x) :=
\iint 
\Big( W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(-x) - W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(u-x) \Big)
e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}\, \tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv).
\]
The function $\Phi$ is the Laplace exponent of a subordinator, which is analytic where finite;
and $q \in \mathcal{D}$ is equivalent
to $\Phi_X(q+q_X)$ being finite and positive.
Moreover, $q\mapsto W_Y^{(q+q_X)}(z)$ has an analytic extension
for each $z$ by Lemma $4$-(ii) in \cite{Bertoin1997}.
This shows that $q \mapsto D^{(q)}_1(x)$ has an analytic extension on~$\mathcal{D}$.

We turn our attention to $D^{(\cdot)}_2(x)$.
Let $\gamma$ be a closed piecewise smooth curve contained in $\mathcal{D}$.  
We will show that $\int_\gamma D^{(q)}_2(x)\, dq =0$.
Consider
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
     \label{eq:D2-integral}
\int_\gamma D^{(q)}_2(x)\, dq
=
\int_\gamma 
\iint 
\Big( W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(u-x)\Big)
e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}
\tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv)\, dq.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
We first control the integrand uniformly in $q$.
Using the analytic extension of scale functions (see Lemma 8.3 in \cite{Kyprianou2014}), for $y, a \in \mathbb{R},$
\[
W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(y)-W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(y-a)
=
\sum_{k\ge 0}(q+q_Y)^k
\Big(W_Y^{*(k+1)}(y)-W_Y^{*(k+1)}(y-a)\Big).
\]
Let $int(\gamma)$ denote the interior of the bounded connected component formed by the closed curve $\gamma$ and define
\[
Q := \sup\{\, |q| : q\in \mathrm{int}(\gamma)\,\}.
\]
Then (using the fact that the convolution of non-negative increasing functions remains increasing),
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\big|W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(y) - W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(y-a)\big|
&\le
\sum_{k\ge 0} (Q+q_Y)^k
\Big(W_Y^{*(k+1)}(y)-W_Y^{*(k+1)}(y-a)\Big)
\nonumber \\
&=
W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(y)-W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(y-a).
\label{eq:mono-diff}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
By \cref{lem:weighted-to-unweighted},
\[
\iint 
\Big(
W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(-x) - W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(-x+u)
\Big)e^{-\Phi_X(\inf \Psi_X)v}
\tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv)
<\infty.
\]
Thus, recalling that $\gamma$ is contained in $\mathcal{D}$,
the integrand in \eqref{eq:D2-integral} is bounded in absolute value by an integrable function
uniformly in $q \in \mathrm{int}(\gamma)$. It also follows from dominated convergence
that $q\mapsto D_2^{(q)}(x)$ is continuous on $\mathcal D$.
Fubini's theorem implies that
\[
\int_\gamma D^{(q)}_2(x)\, dq
=
\iint 
\left( \int_\gamma 
\big( W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(u-x)\big)
e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}dq
\right)
\tilde{\Pi}_X(du, dv).
\]

For each fixed $(u,v)$, the function
\[
q \mapsto 
\big( W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-x)-W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(u-x)\big)
e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}
\]
is analytic on $\mathcal{D}$; hence its integral around $\gamma$ is zero.  
Therefore,
$\int_\gamma D^{(q)}_2(x)\, dq=0$,
and by Morera’s theorem, $D^{(\cdot)}_2(x)$ has analytic extension in~$\mathcal{D}$. Hence, proved.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:Dq_bound}
Fix $\inf \Psi_X-q_X<-Q < 0$ and recall the function $A$ appearing in \cref{lem:weighted-to-unweighted}. Then,
\[
|D^{(q)}(x)| \leq g(Q)W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(-x)+ A(Q+q_Y,\Phi_X(\inf\Psi_X), x),
\qquad -Q\le q<0, \, x\le 0,
\]
where $g$ is a positive function, and the right-hand side is integrable on $(b,0)$.
Moreover,
\[
  |W_{V}^{(q)}(x, y)| \leq W_{V}^{(Q)}(x, y),
  \qquad
  -Q\le q<0, \, x\in \mathbb{R} , \, y < 0.
\]
and the right-hand side is integrable in $y$ on $(b,0)$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We use the same decomposition in \cref{lem:Dq_analytic}, namely $D^{(q)}(x)=D^{(q)}_1(x)+D^{(q)}_2(x)$, and begin with $D_1^{(q)}(x)$.

\citet[Lemma~4(iii)]{Bertoin1997} gives the bound
$|W_Y^{(q+q_Y)}(-x)|\leq W^{(Q+q_Y)}_Y(-x)$.
By Assumption \ref{a:R}, $\Phi_X$ is positive and continuous on $[-Q,\infty)$,
so $g(Q) \coloneqq \sup\bigl\{ \frac{q+q_X}{\Phi_X(q+q_X)} : -Q \le q < 0 \bigr\}$
is finite. This completes the bound on $D_1^{(q)}(x)$.


We turn to $D_2^{(q)}(x)$.
Again using the comparison in \eqref{eq:mono-diff} and monotonicity of $\Phi_X$, we obtain
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    |D^{(q)}_2(x)| \leq
&\int 
\Big| W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(-x) - W^{(q+q_Y)}_Y(u-x) \Big|e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)v}
\, \tilde{\Pi}_X(du\, dv)\\
& \leq \int 
\Big(
W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(-x) - W_Y^{(Q+q_Y)}(-x+u)
\Big)e^{-\Phi_X(\inf \Psi_X)v}
\tilde{\Pi}_X(du\, dv)
\\&= A(Q+q_Y, \Phi_X(\inf\Psi_X), x),
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
which is finite by \cref{lem:weighted-to-unweighted}. This completes the bound on
$D_2^{(q)}(x)$.

Finally we consider $W_V^{(q)}(x, y)$. This follows from the analytic extension in \cref{prop:WV-analytic}, that is
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    |W_V^{(q)}(x, b)| \leq  \sum_{n\geq 0} |q|^n W^{*(n+1)}_V(x, b).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Considering the range of $q$ produces the required bound.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
Let $(S,\mathcal{S})$ be a measurable space, $\varphi$ be a non-trivial,
$\sigma$-finite measure on $(S,\mathcal{S})$ and 
$P = (P_t)_{t\ge 0}$ a transition semigroup on $(S,\mathcal{S})$.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        {\begin{enumerate}} 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\item $P$ is called \emph{$\varphi$-irreducible} if,
for every $x \in S$ and every measurable set $A \in \mathcal{S}$ satisfying
$\varphi(A) > 0$,
\[
\int_0^\infty P_t(x,A)\,dt > 0.
\]
\item 
$P$ is called \emph{simultaneously $\varphi$-irreducible} if, for
every $h>0$, the associated $h$-skeleton chain $(P_{nh})_{n\ge 0}$ is
$\varphi$-irreducible; that is, for every $x\in S$ and every
$A\in\mathcal{S}$ with $\varphi(A)>0$,
\[
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty} P_{nh}(x,A) > 0.
\]
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        {\end{enumerate}} 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{irreducibility}
Under Assumption \ref{a:A1}, for all $x,y>b$,
\[
  \mathbb{P}_x\big( H_y < \tau_b^-\wedge \zeta \big) > 0.
\]
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The proof substantially follows \citet[Proposition 1]{Bertoin1997}, and in part
makes use of that result directly.

To begin with, we note that since $\zeta$ occurs at finite rate, we may retain or
omit it (i.e., consider $V$ or $U$)
from the probability under consideration without changing the question of
positivity, and we so freely in what follows.

Suppose first that $x\le y$. Then
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{upward_passage_positivity}
  \mathbb{P}_x(H_y<\tau_b^-\wedge \zeta)
  = \frac{W_V^{(0)}(x,b)}{W_V^{(0)}(y,b)} > 0,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
since $W_V^{(0)}(\cdot, b)$ is strictly increasing.
For $y<x$, it is sufficient to show that $\mathbb{P}_x(\tau_y^-<\tau_b^-) > 0$.

When $0<y<x$, it is clear that $\mathbb{P}_x(\tau_y^-<\tau_b^-) > 0$, since this reduces
to a question about the Lévy process $X$.

Assume now that, in Assumption (A1), there exists some $b<\ell<0$ in the support
of $\Pi_Y$. When $y<x<0$, \cite[Proposition~1]{Bertoin1997} shows that
\[
  \mathbb{P}_x(H_y<\tau_b^-) \ge \mathbb{P}_x(H_y < \tau_b^-\wedge \tau_0^+) > 0,
\]
since the latter probability concerns just the Lévy process $Y$.
On the other hand when $y<0<x$, we note that by considering just $X$,
$\mathbb{P}_x(\tau_0^- < \tau_b^-)>0$, and on this event we
apply the Markov property at $\tau_0^-$ and reduce to the preceding case.
This concludes the proof under one of the alternatives of Assumption (A1).

Now assume the other alternative of Assumption (A1):
there exists some $\ell<b$ in the support of
$\Pi_X$, and fix $y<x$ with $y<0$, which is the case that remains open.
$\Pi_X(\ell-\varepsilon,\ell+\varepsilon)>0$
for some $0<\varepsilon<y-b$.
Notice that $y-\ell-\varepsilon/2>0$.
Since we have already proved the result when starting from and hitting
points above $0$, we know that
$\mathbb{P}_x(H_{y-\ell-\varepsilon/2} < \tau_b^-)>0$.
On this event, using the a.s.\ right-continuity of paths at \(H_{y - \ell - \varepsilon/2}\), 
for some \(\delta > 0\) we have with positive probability that
\[
  U_s \in (y - \ell - \varepsilon,\, y - \ell), \qquad 
  s \in [H_{y - \ell - \varepsilon/2},\, H_{y - \ell - \varepsilon/2} + \delta].
\]
Within this short interval of time, the process may, again with positive probability,
experience a jump
with size in $(\ell-\varepsilon,\ell+\varepsilon)$ with positive probability.
On this event, \(U\) lands below level \(y\), but above \(b\), before exiting the interval \((b, \infty)\). This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{lebesgue_irr}
Under Assumption \ref{a:A1}, the process $V$ killed upon first passage below $b$ is Lebesgue irreducible.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
It follows from \cref{irreducibility}
that for sufficiently large $L$ and for $x,y\in (b,L)$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:reachability}
    \mathbb{P}_x\big( H_y < \tau_L^+ \wedge \tau_b^-\wedge \zeta \big) > 0.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
The case, when $x<y$, it is clear that the resolvent density in \cref{exist_resolvent} is positive, since $W_V(x, y), W^{(q_X)}(x-y)$ both are identically zero.

So, we are concerned only with the case when $x>y.$ For the case, when $y<0,$
using this type of event a few times (and noticing that one can hit $y<x$ only
by passing below it first) we obtain
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    0 
    < \mathbb{P}_x\big( \tau_b^-\wedge \zeta >\tau_L^+  > \tau_y^-  \big)
    &= \mathbb{P}_x(\tau_b^-\wedge \zeta> \tau_L^+) - \mathbb{P}_x(\tau_y^- \wedge \zeta> \tau_L^+)
    \nonumber \\
    &= \frac{W_{V}(x, b)}{W_{V}(L, b)} - \frac{W_{V}(x, y)}{W_{V}(L, y)},
    \label{eq:WV-comparison}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
with the last line following from the two-sided exit problem, \cref{two-sided-exit}. 

Looking at the resolvent density $r_{(b,L)}^{(0)}$ in \cref{exist_resolvent},
we see that $r_{(b,L)}^{(0)}(x,y) > 0$ for all $b<y<0$.
Similarly, for the case when $y>0,$ we notice that 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \mathbb{P}_x(\tau_y^- \wedge \zeta> \tau_L^+)=\frac{W_X^{(q_X)}(x-y)}{W_X^{(q_X)}(L-y)},
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
using Lévy process arguments, and substitute in \eqref{eq:WV-comparison} to obtain
\[
  \frac{W_{V}(x, b)}{W_{V}(L, b)} > \frac{W_X^{(q_X)}(x-y)}{W_X^{(q_X)}(L-y)}.
\]
Comparing again with the resolvent density, we see that $r_{(b,L)}^{(0)}(x,y)>0$
for $x>y>0$.

Consequently, we have $R^{(0)}_{(b,L)}(x,A) > 0$ for the resolvent applied to
any $A$ of positive
Lebesgue measure. Notice that this proves Lebesgue irreducibility of the
process killed on exiting $(b,L)$; this will be used in a subsequent proof.

We note here, for future use, that these arguments are valid
for $L \ge \max\{-\sup(\operatorname{supp} \Pi_X) + b, 0\}$ 
under \ref{a:A1}\ref{a:A1a}, and for
any $L>0$ under \ref{a:A1}\ref{a:A1b}.


Since $L$ can be chosen arbitrarily large, this completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:positivity_survival_general}
Assume \ref{a:A2}. For any $c<d$, any $x\in (c,d)$ and any time $t>0$,
\[
\mathbb{P}_x\big(T_d^+ \wedge T_c^- > t \big) > 0.
\]
The same result holds with $X$ replaced by $Y$ and \ref{a:A2} replaced by \ref{a:A3}.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Fix $c<d$ and $x\in (c,d)$.
Let us write $H_x^{(n)}$ for the $n$-th hitting time of $x$
by $X$ before exiting the interval $(c,d)$, with $H^{(0)}_x = 0$
and $\{H_x^{(n)}=\infty\}$ denoting the event that $X$ leaves the interval before the $n$-th return to $x$. Under Assumption \ref{a:A2}, \cite[Proposition~1]{Bertoin1997} implies that there exist $h, \epsilon>0$ such that $\mathbb{P}_x\left(H_x\in(h, \infty)\right)>\epsilon.$
For every $t>0$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \mathbb{P}_x(\tau_c^-\wedge \tau_d^+ > t)
    &\geq \mathbb{P}_x\left(H_x^{(n)}-H_x^{(n-1)}\in(h, \infty), \text{ for all } n\leq [t/h]\right)\\
    &\geq \mathbb{P}_x\left(H_x\in (h, \infty)\right)^{[t/h]}>\epsilon^{[t/h]}>0.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:minorization-X}
Suppose Assumptions \ref{a:A2} and \ref{a:A4} hold.
Let \(A\subset(0,\infty)\) be a set of positive Lebesgue measure.
Then for every starting point \(x\geq0\) there exist a time \(t_0=t_0(x,A)>0\),
a function $m^{x,A}\colon (t_0,\infty) \to (0,\infty)$ and a measure
$\mu_A$ on $(0,\infty)$
such that
\[
\mathbb{P}_x\big( X_t \in dz \; ; t<\tau_0^-\big) \ge m^{x,A}(t) \mu_A(dz),
\qquad t > t_0.
\]
Moreover, $\mu_A(A)>0$ and, for any $t_0<t_1<t_2$, $\inf_{[t_1,t_2]} m^{x,A} > 0$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Let \(A \subset (0, \infty)\) be a set of positive Lebesgue measure. 
Fix a small \(\varepsilon > 0\), and partition \((0, \infty)\) into intervals of length 
strictly less than \(\alpha_2 - \alpha_1\). 
There exists at least one interval in this partition, 
say \([a_1,a_2]\), such that $A \cap [a_1,a_2]$ has positive Lebesgue measure.
By construction, $a_2 - a_1 < \alpha_2 - \alpha_1$,
which implies
\[
0 < a_2 + \alpha_1 < a_1 + \alpha_2,
\]
so the open interval $(a_2 + \alpha_1, \, a_1 + \alpha_2)$
is nonempty. Choose an interval  \( I := (\underline{I}, \overline{I}) \), satisfying
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{assum-I}
a_2 + \alpha_1 < \underline{I} < \bar{I} < a_1 + \alpha_2
\quad \text{and} \quad 
\bar{I} - \underline{I} < a_1.    
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
For any $x\ge 0$ and $y \in I$, \citet[Proposition 1]{Bertoin1997} implies that there exists
$t_0 = t_0(x,y,A)$ such that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:minor-1:x-to-y}
  \mathbb{P}_x(H_y < t_0 \wedge \tau_0^-) > 0.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    

We decompose the process \(X\) as
\[
X_t = \widetilde{X}_t + J_t,
\]
where $\tilde{X}$ and $J$ are independent, with $\tilde{X}_0=y$ and $J_0=0$;
\(J\) is a compound Poisson process
whose Lévy measure is the restriction of \(\Pi_X\) to
\([-\alpha_2,-\alpha_1]\).

Let \(N_t^J\) denote the number of jumps of \(J\) up to time \(t\).
Let $\delta = \inf_{u \in (-\alpha_2,-\alpha_1)} \pi_X(u)$,
which is positive by Assumption \ref{a:A4}.
For $y\in I$, \(z \in [a_1,a_2]\) and $t>0$, we compute as follows,
using the fact that $\bar{I}-\underline{I}<a_1$ in the first line:
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\mathbb{P}_y(X_t \in dz\; , \tau_0^->t)
&\ge \mathbb{P}_y\!\left( \tilde{X}_t + J_t \in dz \,,\, N_t^J = 1\;, \tilde{\tau}_I>t \right) \nonumber \\
&\ge \int_I \mathbb{P}_y(\tilde{X}_t \in du\; , \tilde{\tau}_I>t)\, \mathbb{P}(N^J_t=1)\frac{\Pi_X(dz - u)}{\Pi_X([-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1])} \nonumber \\
&= \Pi_X([-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1])te^{-\Pi_X[-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1]t}\int_I \mathbb{P}_y(\tilde{X}_t \in du, \tilde{\tau}_I>t)\, \frac{\Pi_X(dz - u)}{\Pi_X([-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1])}
\nonumber \\
&\ge \delta te^{-\Pi_X[-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1]t}\mathbb{P}_y(\tilde{\tau}_I>t)\, dz.
\label{dubrosin-condition-X-1}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
The last line uses \(z - u \in [\alpha_1, \alpha_2]\), which follows from the
construction of $I$.

The Lévy measure of $\tilde{X}$,
$\Pi_X(\cdot \setminus [-\alpha_2, -\alpha_1])$, still satisfies Assumption \ref{a:A2}.
Therefore, by \cref{lem:positivity_survival_general}, the probability that
\(\tilde{X}\) remains within \(I\) up to \(t>0\) can be chosen arbitrarily
large, is also strictly positive, that is,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:tilde-survival-positive}
  \mathbb{P}_y(\tilde{\tau}_I > t) > 0, \qquad y\in I.  
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    

We now consider starting at $x\ge 0$ not necessarily in $I$.
By the strong Markov property at $H_y$, for every $t>t_0$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\mathbb{P}_x(X_t\in dz;\,t<\tau_0^-)
&\geq
\int_{[0,t_0)}
\mathbb{P}_x(H_y\in ds,\,H_y<\tau_0^-)
\mathbb{P}_y(X_{t-s}\in dz;\,t-s<\tau_0^-).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Combining this with \eqref{dubrosin-condition-X-1}, we obtain
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{dubrosin-condition-X}
    \mathbb{P}_x(X_t\in dz;\,t<\tau_0^-)
    \geq m^{x,A}(t)\mathbf{1}_{A\cap[a_1,a_2]}(z)\,dz
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
where
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
m^{x,A}(t)
=
\delta\int_{[0,t_0)}
(t-s)e^{-\Pi_X[-\alpha_2,-\alpha_1](t-s)}
\mathbb{P}_y(\widetilde\tau_I>t-s)
\mathbb{P}_x(H_y\in ds,\,H_y<\tau_0^-).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
By \eqref{eq:minor-1:x-to-y} and
\eqref{eq:tilde-survival-positive}, $m^{x,A}(t)>0$ for every $t>t_0$.

Finally, if $t\in[t_1,t_2]$ with $t_1>t_0$, then
$t-s\in[t_1-t_0,t_2]$ for $s\in[0,t_0)$. Therefore,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
m^{x,A}(t)
&\geq
\delta
\inf_{r\in[t_1-t_0,t_2]}
\bigl(re^{-\lambda r}\bigr)
\mathbb{P}_y(\widetilde\tau_I>t_2)
\mathbb{P}_x(H_y<t_0\wedge\tau_0^-) > 0.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Since the right-hand side is independent of $t\in[t_1,t_2]$ this concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:minorization-Y}
Suppose Assumptions \ref{a:A3} and \ref{a:A5} hold.
Let \(A\subset(b, 0)\) be a set of positive Lebesgue measure.
Then for every starting point \(x\in(b, 0)\) there exists a time \(t_0=t_0(x,A)>0\),
a function $\tilde{m}^{x,A}\colon (t_0,\infty) \to (0,\infty)$ and a measure
$\tilde{\mu}_A$ on $(b, 0)$
such that
\[
\mathbb{P}_x\big( Y_t \in dz \; ; t<\tau_0^+\wedge\tau_b^-\big) \ge \tilde{m}^{x,A}(t) \tilde{\mu}_A(dz),
\qquad t > t_0.
\]
Moreover, $\tilde{\mu}_A(A)>0$ and, for any $t_0<t_1<t_2$, $\inf_{[t_1,t_2]} \tilde{m}^{x,A} > 0$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Recall that Assumption~\ref{a:A5} implies
Assumption~\ref{a:A1}, so we are free to use results proved under
the latter condition.
Let \(A \subset (b,0)\) be a measurable set of positive Lebesgue measure. By the Lebesgue density theorem, there exists a density point
\[
a_1 \in A, \qquad b<a_1<\sup A \le 0,
\]
such that $A\cap[a_1,a_2]$ has positive Lebesgue measure for every $a_2>a_1$.
Using \ref{a:A5}, let $0<\gamma_1<\gamma_2$ be such that $\delta\coloneqq \inf_{[-\gamma_2,-\gamma_1]}\pi_Y>0$
and $a_1+\gamma_2<0$.
Now take any $a_2$ with the property that
\[
  a_1 < a_2 < a_1+\gamma_2-\gamma_1.
\]
With these conditions in place, choose an interval $I = (\underline{I},\bar{I}) \subset (b,0)$
such that $\bar{I}-\underline{I}<a_1-b$ and 
\[
  a_2+\gamma_1 \le \underline{I} < \bar{I} \le a_1+\gamma_2.
\]
Having established the existence of intervals $I$ and $[a_1,a_2]$ with these properties,
the proof proceeds almost identically to that of \cref{lem:minorization-X}
(albeit using \cref{irreducibility} to go from starting points in $I$ to ones in $(b,0)$)
and therefore we omit it.
We note that the difference between the proofs lies in the fact that $a_1$ and $\gamma_2$
depend on the set $A$, which explains the fact that \ref{a:A5} is stronger than
the corresponding assumption on $X$. \qedhere
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{sim-leb-irr}
    Under Assumptions (A2--5), the process $V$ killed upon going below $b$ is simultaneously Lebesgue irreducible.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Initially, fix a set $A\subset (0, \infty)$ of positive Lebesgue measure.
        Start by considering $x>0$. 
        From \cref{lem:minorization-X}, there exists $t_0 = t_0(x,A)$ such that
        for $t>t_0$,
        
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
           \mathbb{P}_x(V_t\in A; \;t<\tau_0^-)>0.
        
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
       (The presence of killing at time $\zeta$ does not affect this result.)
       When $x \in (b,0]$, applying \cref{irreducibility} allows us to reduce to the
       case of starting above zero. Again similar argument holds for $A \subset (b, 0)$ by \cref{lem:minorization-Y}.
       
       This will imply that for $x\in (b, 0),$ there exists a time \(t_0=t_0(x,A)>0\) such that
for all \(t>t_0\)
\[
\mathbb{P}_x\big( V_t \in A \; ; t<\tau_0^+\wedge \tau_b^-\big) > 0.
\] 
Finally, take $x \in [0, \infty),$ we again use \eqref{upward_passage_positivity} and iteratively, we have the following. That is, for all $x \in (b, \infty)$ and any set $A\subset(b, \infty)$ of Lebesgue positive measure ($\{0\}$ is ignored as it is Lebesgue zero set), there exits $t(x, A)$ such that for all $t>t(x, A):$
\[
\mathbb{P}_x\big( V_t \in A \; ; t<\tau_b^-\big) > 0.
\] 
Thus, we have that for all $h>0$, $x\in (b, \infty)$ and positive Lebesgue measure set $A\subset (b, \infty)$, the $0$-resolvents are positive.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{unifrom-minorizing-measure}
Under the Assumptions \ref{a:A1}--\ref{a:A5}, for a bounded Borel set $L\subset (b, \infty)$ with $\inf L > b$
and for any set $A\subset(b, \infty)$ of positive Lebesgue measure, there exist $t_1=t_1(L, A)$,
a function $m^{L,A}\colon (t_1,\infty) \to (0,\infty)$ and a measure $\mu'_A$ on $(b,\infty)$
such that for all $t>t_1$, $y\in A$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \inf_{x\in L}\mathbb{P}_x(V_t\in dy; \; t<\tau_b^-)\geq m^{L, A}(t)\mu_A'(dy).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Moreover $\inf_{[t_2, t_3]}m^{L,A}>0$ for $t_1 < t_2 < t_3$ and $\mu'_A(A) > 0$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
We consider separately the cases
$A\subset (0,\infty)$ and $A\subset (b,0)$.
We first consider $A\subset (0,\infty)$ and assume that
\[
L=[d_1,d_2]\subset (b,\infty), \qquad d_1>b \text{ and } d_2>0,
\]
which can be done by possibly enlarging $L$, without loss of generality.
Next, since $W_V(x,b)>0$ for all $x\in (b,\infty)$, it follows from
\cref{two-sided-exit} that there exists $t_L>0$
such that
\[
\mathbb{P}_{d_1}\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right)>0.
\]
For fixed $x\in L$,
applying the strong Markov property at $\tau_x^+$,
\[
\mathbb{P}_{d_1}\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right)
\le
\mathbb{P}_{d_1}\!\left(
\tau_x^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right)
\,
\mathbb{P}_x\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right),
\]
which yields
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{inf-lower-bound-clean}
\inf_{x\in L}\mathbb{P}_x\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right)
\ge
\mathbb{P}_{d_1}\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<\tau_{b}^-\wedge t_L\wedge \zeta
\right)
>0,
\qquad x\in L.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Applying \cref{lem:minorization-X} and additionally accounting for the killing at time $\zeta$,
there exists $t_0=t_0(d_2,A)>0$ such that for all $t>t_0$,
\[
\mathbb{P}_{d_2}(V_t\in dy,\ t<\tau_0^-\wedge \zeta)
\ge
e^{-q_X t} m^{d_2, A}(t)\mu_A(dy),
\qquad y\in A,
\]
for some $m^{d_2, A}(t)>0$. 


Let $t_1 = t_L+t_0(d_2,A)$ and take $t> t_1$ and $x\in L$.
Applying the strong Markov property at $\tau_{d_2}^+$,
we obtain
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
&\mathbb{P}_x(V_t \in dy ,\, t<\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta)
\\&\ge 
\mathbb{P}_x\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<t_L\wedge\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta,\;
t<\tau_b^-\wedge \zeta,\;
V_t\in dy
\right)
\\
&=
\mathbb{E}_x\!\left[
\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{d_2}^+<t_L\wedge\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta\}}
\bigl. \mathbb{P}_{d_2}\!\left(
V_{t-s}\in dy,\;
t-s<\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta
\right)\bigr\rvert_{s=\tau_{d_2}^+}
\right]
\\
&\ge
\mathbb{E}_x\!\left[
e^{-q(t-\tau_{d_2}^+)}
\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{d_2}^+<t_L\wedge\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta\}}
m^{d_2,A}(t-\tau_{d_2}^+)\right]
\mu^A(dy)
\\
&\ge
e^{-q_Xt}\mathbb{P}_x\!\left(
\tau_{d_2}^+<t_L\wedge\tau_b^- \wedge \zeta
\right)
\inf_{0\le \gamma\le t_L+t_0(d_2, A)}
m^{d_2, A}(t-\gamma)\,\mu_A(dy).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    

Combining this with \eqref{inf-lower-bound-clean}, we deduce that
\[
\inf_{x\in L}\mathbb{P}_x(V_t\in dy;\ t<\tau_b^-\wedge \zeta)
\ge m^{L, A}(t)\,\mu_A(dy),
\]
for some $m^{L, A}(t)>0$. Moreover, the positivity
$\inf_{[t_2,t_3]} m^{L,A} > 0$ for $t_1<t_2<t_3$
is inherited from the corresponding property of $m^{d_2, A}$.

The case $A\subset (b,0)$ is treated in an analogous manner. The only difference is that we now take $L=[d_1,d_2]$ with $d_1<0$.
In this case, we use \cref{irreducibility} to replace \eqref{inf-lower-bound-clean} by the inequality
\[
  \inf_{x\in L} \mathbb{P}_x(H_{d_1}<\tau^-_b \wedge t_L \wedge \zeta)
  \ge \mathbb{P}_{d_1}(\tau^+_{d_2} \vee H_{d_1} < \tau^-_b \wedge t_L \wedge\zeta) > 0,
\]
and then proceed as in the proof above, using $d_1$ as a reference point instead of $d_2$,
and \cref{lem:minorization-Y} in place of \cref{lem:minorization-X}.

    \item The resolvent density $r^{(q)}_b$ obtained in \cref{lem:limit_potential}
remains valid for $q > \max\{\, \inf \Psi_X-q_X,\, -\sigma(b)\}$.
  \item The resolvent density $r^{(q)}_{(b,a)}$ found in \cref{exist_resolvent} remains valid for $q>-\sigma(b, a)$.
  \item\label{i:cor_1:3}
  If \ref{a:A1}\ref{a:A1a} holds, let $L \ge \max\{-\sup(\operatorname{supp} \Pi_X) + b, 0\}$,
  and if \ref{a:A1}\ref{a:A1b} holds, let $L>0$.
  Then, 
  $\sigma(b, x) \ge \sigma(b,a)$ for all $a\ge L$ and $x \le a$.

\item
Start by taking a non-negative, bounded measurable function $f$, supported on $(0, \infty)$.
We know from
\cref{lem:limit_potential} 
that for $q\geq 0,$ for a fixed $x \in \mathbb{R}$ we have:
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {

    }

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{local-resolvent-eqn}
\mathbb{E}_x\!\left[
\int_0^{\tau_b^-}
e^{-qt} f(V_t)\,dt
\right]
=
\int_{(b,\infty)} f(y)\, r_{b}^{(q)}(x,y)\, dy.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
From \cref{lem:Dq_analytic} and \cref{prop:WV-analytic},
it can be seen that $r_b^{(q)}(x,y)$ is analytic for $q > \max\{\, \inf \Psi_X-q_X,\, -\sigma(b)\}$. Now, fix $\max\{\, \inf \Psi_X-q_X,\, -\sigma(b) \,\}<q_0<0$.
By assumption (R), we know that there exists $\epsilon = \operatorname*{arg\,inf}\psi_X >0$ such that
$e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)y}\leq e^{-\epsilon y}$ for $q\ge q_0$ and $y\ge 0$.
Also, note that $C\coloneqq\inf_{0\le q\le q_0}D^{(q)}(b)>0,$ since $D^{(\cdot)}(b)$ is analytic
by \cref{lem:Dq_analytic}. Therefore, for fixed $x$ and $y>0$, we have an analytic extension of the above as follows:
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    r^{(q)}_b(x, y) = \sum_{n\geq 0} q^n d_n(x, y)
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Therefore, using \cref{lem:Dq_bound}
and the expression for $r_b^{(q)}$ in \cref{lem:limit_potential}, we have 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}}

    }

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    &\lvert r_b^{(q)}(x,y) \rvert
= \left|\frac{e^{-\Phi_X(q+q_X)y}}{D^{(q)}(b)}W_{V}^{(q)}(x,b) \,  - W^{(q+q_X)}_X(x-y)\right|\\
&
\leq \frac{1}{C}e^{-\epsilon y}W_V^{(q_0)}(x, b)+ W^{(q_0+q_X)}_X(x-y),
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
for $q\ge q_0$ and $y\ge 0$.
Since the right-hand side is non-negative and integrable in $y$, we can use Fubini's theorem, to conclude that the integral on the right-hand-side of \eqref{local-resolvent-eqn} also has an extension to $q>q_0,$ and therefore for all $q > \max\{\, \inf \Psi_X-q_X,\, -\sigma(b)\}$. The rest of the argument follows exactly as in Proposition $3$ in \cite{Bertoin1997}
and this concludes the proof for $y > 0$.

For $y<0$, the same argument works with a slight modification to ensure integrability,
as follows.
Assume $q_0\le q\le 0$ where $-\max\{\psi_X-q_X,-\sigma(b)\} < q_0 < 0$ and fix $y\in (b,0)$.
We have by \cref{lem:Dq_bound} that $D^{(q)}\rvert_{(0,b)}$
is bounded (uniformly in such $q$) by an integrable function.
     
                              
                          
                                                                                       
    
                                                                                                                                                                                    

                                                                                                                                                     
                                                        
                                 
               
                     
        
                                                                                                   
                               
                                
                                                                                       
                           
             
                                                                                                                                                                 
           
                   
                                                                                    
                   
      
        
                                                                                    
                   
                 
                                                                         


\item\label{i:cor_1:2}
This is similar to the preceding part, and even a little simpler,
since now $r_{(b,a)}$ is written in
terms of $W_V^{(q)}$ and $W_X^{(q)}$ alone.
We use the bounds on $W_V^{(q)}(x,b)$ from \cref{lem:Dq_bound}
along with classical bounds on the scale functions of $X$.
Since $W_{V}^{(q)}(x, b)$ is analytic in $q$ from \cref{prop:WV-analytic}, these show
that the resolvent representation of \cref{exist_resolvent} remains valid for all \(q > -\sigma(b,a)\).

\item
As remarked at the end of \cref{lebesgue_irr}, for such $L$, the
process killed on exiting $(b, L)$ is Lebesgue irreducible;
see the end of the proof of \cref{lebesgue_irr}.
Let $a\ge L$ and suppose, for contradiction, that, for some \(q < \sigma(b,a)\) and \(x_0 \in (b,a)\),  \(W_V^{(-q)}(x_0,b) = 0\).

Then, by part \ref{i:cor_1:2} and \cref{exist_resolvent}, we see that $r^{(-q)}_{(b,a)}(x_0, y) = 0$ for all $y\in (x_0, a),$ since the first term in $r^{(-q)}_{(b,a)}(x_0, y)$ is zero by the assumption, and the second term $W^{(-q+q_X)}(x_0-y) = 0$ since $y>x_0.$
This contradicts the Lebesgue irreducibility of \(V\).
Hence \(W_V^{(-q)}(x,b) > 0\) for all \(x \in (b,a)\), which establishes the claim.
